\displaystyle|Q_{2}|\leq C_{\varepsilon_{5}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{5}\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right).

Combining (
3.51
) and (
3.52
) leads to

(3.53)

\displaystyle-\int\dive[2\mu D(v)]\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)+\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-C_{\varepsilon_{6}}\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon_{6}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\varepsilon_{6}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right).

Next, using Lemma
2.1
and
3.1
again, we estimate
M_{1}-M_{4}
, that is,

(3.54)

\displaystyle\left|M_{1}\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon_{7}}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{7}\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right),

(3.55)

\displaystyle\left|M_{2}\right|

\displaystyle=2c_{0}\left|\int\mu^{\prime}\partial_{j}\rho\partial_{ij}\rho^{-1}(v_{t})_{i}+\int\mu(\rho)\partial_{ijj}\rho^{-1}(v_{t})_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=2c_{0}\left|\int\mu^{\prime}\partial_{j}\rho\partial_{ij}\rho^{-1}(v_{t})_{i}-\int\mu^{\prime}\partial_{i}\rho\partial_{jj}\rho^{-1}(v_{t})_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int(\left|\nabla\rho\right|^{3}+\left|\nabla\rho\right||\nabla^{2}\rho|)\left|v_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{8}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{8}\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

(3.56)

\displaystyle\left|M_{3}+M_{4}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|c_{0}\int\partial_{j}\left(u_{j}\partial_{i}\log\rho\right)(v_{t})_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|c_{0}\int\partial_{j}\left(\log\rho\partial_{i}v_{j}\right)(v_{t})_{i}+c_{0}^{2}\int\partial_{j}\left(\log\rho\partial_{ij}\rho^{-1}\right)(v_{t})_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int\left(\left|\nabla v\right|\left|\nabla\rho\right|+\left|\nabla\rho\right|^{3}+\left|\nabla\rho\right|\left|\nabla^{2}\rho\right|\right)\left|v_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{9}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{9}\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Combining (
3.53
)–(
3.56
), we have, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2]
, there exists a positive constant
C_{\varepsilon}
depending on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\varepsilon
such that

(3.57)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)+\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)F(t)+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

For general boundary case, that is,
\curle v=-n^{\perp}\cdot B\cdot(v+c_{0}\nabla\rho^{-1})
, it suffices to calculate the following extra term

(3.58)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial}\phi(\rho)v_{t}\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial}\phi(\rho)(v_{t}\cdot n^{\perp})n^{\perp}\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho

\displaystyle=\int(v_{t}\cdot n^{\perp})\nabla^{\perp}\phi(\rho)\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho+\int\phi(\rho)(v_{t}\cdot n^{\perp})\nabla^{\perp}\cdot(B\cdot\nabla\rho)

\displaystyle+\int\phi(\rho)\nabla^{\perp}(v_{t}\cdot n^{\perp})\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}G_{i},

where
\phi(s):=c_{0}\mu(s)s^{-2}
. For the first two terms of (
3.58
), using Lemma
2.1
and
3.1
, we have

(3.59)

\left|G_{1}+G_{2}\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{1}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

For
G_{3}
, since we can not handle the term
\nabla^{\perp}(v_{t}\cdot n^{\perp})
, it shall be converted into

\displaystyle G_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)-\int\phi(\rho)_{t}\nabla^{\perp}(v\cdot n^{\perp})\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho-\int\phi(\rho)\nabla^{\perp}(v\cdot n^{\perp})\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho_{t}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)-\left[C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{2}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\right].

Thus, combining (
3.60
)–(
3.57
), we deduce the estimate which is simliar with (
3.57
), that is

(3.60)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)+\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{A}_{4}(t)F(t)+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{A}_{5}(t),

for some integrable functions
\mathcal{A}_{4}
and
\mathcal{A}_{5}
defined on
[0,\infty)
.

We still need estimate
\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}
. Let us rewrite (
3.49
) as

(3.61)

-\mu\Delta v+\nabla\pi=F,

where

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle:=-\rho v_{t}+\nabla(\log\rho)_{t}-\rho u\cdot\nabla v+2\mu^{\prime}\nabla\rho\cdot D(v)+2c_{0}\dive\left(\mu\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle-c_{0}\dive{\left(\rho v\otimes\nabla\rho^{-1}\right)}-c_{0}^{2}\dive{\left(\rho\nabla\rho^{-1}\otimes\nabla\rho^{-1}\right)}

Since
\mu(\rho(x,t))
is bounded contiuous on
\overline{Q}_{T}
from Lemma
2.7
, it follows from Lemma
2.8
with
\varphi=-n^{\perp}\cdot B\cdot(v+c_{0}\nabla\rho^{-1})
that

(3.62)

\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\left(\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\varphi\right\|_{H^{1}}\right),

where

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi\right\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{4}}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\||v|\!\cdot\!|\nabla v|\right\|_{L^{2}}+\||\nabla\rho|\!\cdot\!|\nabla v|\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{6}}^{3}

\displaystyle+\||\nabla\rho|\!\cdot\!|\nabla^{2}\rho|\|_{L^{2}}+\||v|\!\cdot\!|\nabla^{2}\rho|\|_{L^{2}}+\||v|\!\cdot\!|\nabla\rho|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Hence, using the Poincaré’s inequality, we deduce from (
3.62
) that

(3.63)

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+C\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

which gives (
3.46
). Finally, substituting (
3.46
) into (
3.60
), we obtain (
3.45
) and complete the proof of the lemma.

For
(\rho,v)
satisfying condition (
B’
), we first convert (
3.49
) into

(3.64)

\displaystyle\rho v_{t}-\dive{[2\mu D(v)]}+\nabla\pi_{1}

\displaystyle=-\rho v\cdot\nabla v+c_{0}\nabla\log\rho\cdot\nabla v+c_{0}\dive{\left[2\mu\rho^{-1}\left(\nabla^{2}\log\rho-\nabla\log\rho\otimes\nabla\log\rho\right)\right]}

\displaystyle+c_{0}\dive{\left(v\otimes\nabla\log\rho\right)}-c_{0}^{2}\dive{\left(\rho^{-1}\nabla\log\rho\otimes\nabla\log\rho\right)}.

Then, following the calculations from (
3.49
) to (
3.57
) and from (
3.61
) to (
3.63
), we can derive the similar estimates (even much easier, since
v_{t}
is vanished on the boundary), that is,

(3.65)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\mu D(v)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\log\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\log\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right),

(3.66)

\displaystyle\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\log\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\log\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\log\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

and, using Lemma
2.8
with
\Phi=0
, together with (
3.66
),

(3.67)

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\log\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\Delta\log\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla(\log\rho)_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

Thus, we complete the proof by plugging (
3.67
) into (
3.65
).
∎

Now, combining Lemma
3.9
–
3.11
, we can complete the proof of Proposition
3.8
.

Proof of Proposition
3.8
.

We first prove the case when
(\rho,v)
satisfies the condition (
A’
). Using Lemma
2.2
, (
3.29
) in Lemma
3.9
and (
3.45
), (
3.46
) in Lemma
3.11
leads to

(3.68)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)\right)+\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(t)F(t)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(t),

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(t)\left(\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)\right)+\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}(t),

where
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
are positive integrable functions defined on
[0,\infty)
. Using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and Lemma
2.2
once again, we deduce the bound

(3.69)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}F(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\|v_{0}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|v_{0}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+1)\leq C,

where we have used, denote by
\rho_{t,0}=\rho_{t}(x,0)
,

\left\|\rho_{t,0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|v_{0}\right\|_{H^{1}}\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|_{H^{2}},

\mathcal{M}_{1}\geq 0
for
B
positively semi-definited and

\left|e^{\int_{0}^{T}h(t)\,dt}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)e^{-\int_{0}^{t}h(s)\,ds}\,dt\right|\leq\varepsilon\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
h(t)
is an integrable function on
[0,\infty)
.

Next, integrating (
3.46
) over
[0,T]
and using the bound (
3.69
) gives

(3.70)

\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq C,

which shows (
3.28
).

To prove the case when
(\rho,v)
satisfies the condition (
B’
), we use (
3.31
) in Lemma
3.9
and (
3.47
) in Lemma
3.11
. It follows from Lemma
3.1
and

2\int|D(v)|^{2}=\int|\nabla v|^{2}

that

(3.71)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|(\log\rho)_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla(\log\rho)_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}(t)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|(\log\rho)_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\Delta\log\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

for some nonegative integrable functions
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{3}
. Using the Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality, one has the bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla v\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|(\log\rho)_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla(\log\rho)_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt

(3.72)

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\|v_{0}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|v_{0}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\left\|\rho_{0}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+1)\leq C,

With the aid of the estimate (
3.30
), (
3.32
), (
3.48
) and (
3.72
), one has

(3.73)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\Delta\log\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\log\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq C.

At last, noticing that

\Delta\rho=\rho\Delta\log\rho+\rho^{-1}|\nabla\rho|^{2},

and

\nabla\Delta\rho=\nabla\rho\Delta\log\rho+\rho\nabla\Delta\log\rho-\rho^{-2}\nabla\rho|\nabla\rho|^{2}+2\rho^{-1}\nabla\rho\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho,

we complete the proof of (
3.28
).
∎

4
A Priori Estimates (II): Case (
C
)

In this section, we will prove Theorem
1.5
via a different approach. The main difficulty lines here is that, in this situation,
v\cdot n=0
and
v=-c_{0}\nabla\rho^{-1}
on
\partial\Omega
, which makes one impossible to handle the high order derivatives of
\rho
appeared in the boundary integrals when we deal with the energy estimates of
v
.

To over come it, we may take a different decomposition on
u
. This idea mainly comes from Lemma
2.4
, which pushes us to introduce a new function
Q=\mathcal{B}[c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1}]
to eliminate the non-divergence-free condition on
u
. More precisely, we split
u
into two parts,
u=w+Q
, and one can find that
w
possesses the nice properties, that is,
w
is divergence-free and
w|_{\partial\Omega}=0
. Therefore, we can use
w
to get the energy estimates for system
\eqref{equation1.1}_{2}
.

Fortunately, in spite of this difficulty, we still has the estimates on
\rho
, which has been derived in Section
3
and
3.3
such as (
3.9
), (
3.39
), etc. This is because those estimates only require
v\cdot n=0
on
\Omega
. Then, using the relation
v=u-c_{0}\nabla\rho^{-1}
, one can easily change the norm of
v
into that of
u
and
\rho
.

Before giving the proof of Theorem
1.5
, we make some comments on the analysis in this section. In this section, we devote to establish the higher order estimates for
(\rho,u)
. One should notice that, if
Q=\mathcal{B}[c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1}]
, then
Q_{t}=\mathcal{B}[c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1}_{t}]
. With this fact, using Remark
2.3
and Lemma
2.4
, one has the following estimates,

(4.1)

\begin{cases}\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{p}},\\
\left\|Q\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right),\\
\left\|Q_{t}\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\||\rho_{t}||\nabla\rho|\right\|_{L^{p}}\right);\end{cases}

for
1<p<\infty
and some constants
C
depending only on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
and
p
. In addition, the following equivalence will be used frequently, that is,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\sim\left\|v\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{p}},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2p}}^{2}

\displaystyle\sim\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2p}}^{2}.

Now, we turn to the proof. The key of the proof is deriving the following proposition. Using the idea from
[
22
]
, we first assume the bounds (
4.3
) and obtain the a priori estimates of
(\rho,u)
, see Lemma
4.2
–
4.3
. Then, these bounds lead to smaller ones (
4.4
) provided
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}
suitably small, which means that we can close the energy estimates of
(\rho,u)
and, consequently, we complete the proof of the theorem.

Proposition 4.1
.

Suppose that
(\rho,u,\pi)
is a smooth solution of (
1.1
). There exists a positive constant
\delta
depending only on
\Omega
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
c_{0}
such that, if
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta
and

(4.3)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\leq 2,\quad\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\right)\,dt\leq 2\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

then the following estimates hold

(4.4)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\leq 1,\quad\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\right)\,dt\leq\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

In order to prove Proposition
4.1
, we need the following estimates.

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose that
(\rho,u,\pi)
is a smooth solution of (
1.1
). There exists some positive constant
C
depending on
\Omega
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
c_{0}
such that, for all
T\in(0,\infty)
,
\alpha\leq\rho\leq\beta
and

(4.5)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\rho-(\rho_{0})_{\Omega}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,dt\leq\left\|\rho_{0}-(\rho_{0})_{\Omega}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Furthermore, if
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
and (
4.3
) holds, one has

(4.6)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Lemma 4.3
.

Suppose that
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
and (
4.3
) is established, then one has

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{F}(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mathcal{G}(t)+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq C\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
\lambda
and
C
are positive constants depending on
\Omega
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
c_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(t):=\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(t):=\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Remark 4.4
.

One should keep in mind that we always have

(4.8)

\left\|\rho_{0}-(\rho_{0})_{\Omega}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}.

We temporarily assume that Lemma
4.2
–
4.3
are established and prove Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Since Lemma
4.3
is established, we have, for some
C_{1}>0
depending only on
\Omega
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\leq C_{1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{1}C\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1

provided

(4.9)

\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta_{1}:=(C_{1}C)^{-1},

and, by Lemma
2.1
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\right)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\leq\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

provided

(4.10)

\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta_{2}:=C^{-1},

where
C
is the constant in Lemma
4.3
.

Thus, if we choose

\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta:=\min\left\{1,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\right\},

then the proof of Proposition
4.1
is completed.
∎

Now, we trun back to prove Lemma
4.2
–
4.3
. Since Lemma
4.2
has already been proved in Section
3
, we only give the proof for Lemma
4.3
.

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

We start with the lower order estimate of
u
. Multiplying
w
on the both sides of
\eqref{equation1.1}_{2}
and integrating over
\Omega
, one has

(4.11)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\rho|u|^{2}+\int 2\mu|D(u)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\rho u_{t}\cdot Q+\int\rho u\cdot\nabla u\cdot Q-\int\dive[2\mu D(u)]\cdot Q

\displaystyle=\int\rho u_{t}\cdot Q+\int\rho u\cdot\nabla u\cdot Q+\int 2\mu D(u)\cdot\nabla Q

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}S_{i},

where, using estimates (
4.1
), Lemma
2.1
and
3.1
,

(4.12)

\begin{cases}|S_{1}|\leq C\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}},\\
|S_{2}|\leq C\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{4}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\\
|S_{3}|\leq C\left\|\nabla Q\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{3}}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\right)+\varepsilon_{3}\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\end{cases}

Here, we still use the notation
\varepsilon_{i}\in(0,1/2]
and the constant
C_{\varepsilon_{i}}
as before. Combining (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) leads to,
\exists\,\nu>0
,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

For the estimate of
u_{t}
, multiplying
w_{t}
on the both sides of
\eqref{equation1.1}_{2}
, one has

(4.14)

\displaystyle\int\rho|u_{t}|^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\int\mu|D(u)|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int\rho u_{t}\cdot Q_{t}-\int\rho u\cdot\nabla u\cdot w_{t}

\displaystyle+\int\mu_{t}|D(u)|^{2}-\int\dive[2\mu D(u)]\cdot Q_{t}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{4}U_{i}.

Using Lemma
2.1
and
3.1
, (
4.1
)–(
4.3
) and Poincaré’s inequality, we have

(4.15)

\left\|Q_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and, thus,

(4.16)

\begin{cases}|U_{1}|&\!\!\!\!\leq C\left\|Q_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}},\\
|U_{2}|&\!\!\!\!\leq C\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|w_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\!\!\!\!\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\!\!\!\!\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{3}\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\\
|U_{3}|&\!\!\!\!\leq C\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{4}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{4}\left\|\Delta u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\\
&\!\!\!\!\leq C_{\varepsilon_{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{4}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right),\\
|U_{4}|&\!\!\!\!\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\!\!\!\!\leq C_{\varepsilon_{5}}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon_{5}}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{5}\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{cases}

Thus, combining (
4.14
) and (
4.16
), one has

(4.17)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{\mu}D(u)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

From the observation of (
4.17
), one have to derive the estimate of
\Delta u
, or that of
\Delta v
. Unfortunately, we can not directly use, for example, (
3.63
) in the Section
3.3
. The main obstacle here is that (
3.63
) strongly depends on the conitnuity of
\rho
and we have not closed the lower bounds of
v
yet, so that we can not apply Lemma
2.7
–
2.8
(notice that Lemma
2.7
requiring
v\in L^{s}(0,T;L^{r})
). Consequently, we apply Lemma
2.9
with
\Phi=-c_{0}\nabla\rho^{-1}
on

(4.18)

-\dive[2\mu D(v)]+\nabla\pi=F,

where

\displaystyle F=-\rho u_{t}-\rho u\cdot\nabla u+c_{0}\dive\left(2\mu\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right)

and, using condition (
4.3
), Lemma
2.1
and
3.1
and Poincaré’s inequality,

(4.19)

\displaystyle\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}},

where we have applied the estimate (
4.2
) and

(4.20)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\quad\text{use }\eqref{8.4}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta v\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Then, using Lemma
2.1
, Lemma
2.9
and Poincaré’s inequality, combining the condition (
4.3
) and the estimates (
4.19
), we can derive a similar estimate of (
3.63
), that is,

\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\pi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Using again (
4.20
) we derive that

(4.21)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\pi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Then, substituting (
4.21
) into (
4.17
),
\exists\,\nu>0
,

(4.22)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\right)\mathcal{F}(t)

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Next, in order to close the estimate (
4.22
), we turn to get the bounds of
\nabla\rho_{t}
and
\nabla\Delta\rho
. From the estimate (
3.39
) and

\displaystyle\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\quad\text{use }\eqref{8.4}

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}})

we have

(4.23)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

On the other hand, for
\nabla\Delta\rho
, since the estimate (
3.35
) we derived in the Section
3.3
is still valid, replacing
v
by
u
and
\nabla\rho
, one has

(4.24)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Using this inequality together with (
4.21
), we eliminate term
\Delta u
and, then, we subsititute it, alonging with(
4.23
), into (
4.22
) to deduce that

(4.25)

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}(t)+\nu\mathcal{G}(t)\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\mathcal{F}(t).

Since we have
\left\|\nabla\rho_{0}\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}
from Remark
1.11
, applying Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality for (
4.25
) and using Lemma
4.2
, we have

(4.26)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{F}(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{G}(t)\,dt\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\right)\leq C\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which, turning back to (
4.21
) to get the bound for
\pi
, implies the estimate (
4.7
).
Therefore, we complete the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

5
Proof of Theorem
1.6

In this section, we devote to accomplish the proofs of Theorem
1.6
in several steps. Our proofs are basically relying on the approach in
[
11
]
. In subsection
5.1
, we are going to solve the linearized system and give some basic uniform estimates, which is critical for the existence proofs in next few subsections. Next, in subsection
5.2
–
5.3
, we will construct an approximate system and use the contraction mapping theorem to show that it admits a unique smooth solution. Finally, in subsection
5.4
, we will prove Theorem
1.6
.

5.1
Linearized Problem

Consider the following linearized problem

(L.P.)

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+\Phi\cdot\nabla\rho-\dive(\varphi^{-1}\nabla\rho)=0,\\
\rho u_{t}+\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla u-\dive(2\mu D)+\nabla p=0,\\
\dive u=c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1},\\
\rho|_{t=0}=\rho_{0},\quad u|_{t=0}=u_{0},\\
\alpha\leq\rho_{0}\leq\beta,\,\,(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in[C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})]^{4}\text{ satisfying }\eqref{compat},\\
(\rho,u)\text{ satisfies one of the bundary conditions }\eqref{A}-\eqref{C},\end{cases}

where
\int p=0
,
\mu=\mu(x,t)\in H^{1}(0,T;C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega}))
is a positive function.

Lemma 5.1

(Linearized problem)
.

Assume that the hypotheses of Theorem
1.6
are satisfied by the data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
. If
\Phi
and
\varphi
satisifes the following conditions

(5.1)

\begin{cases}\Phi\in C([0,T];H^{1})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}),\quad\Phi_{t}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}),\\
\varphi\in C([0,T];H^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{3}),\quad\varphi_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}),\\
c^{-1}\leq\varphi\leq c,\quad\dive\Phi=0\text{ in }\Omega,\end{cases}

then there exists a unique global strong solution
(\rho,u,p)
to the problem (
L.P.
) satisfying (
1.10
).

Proof.

We only give the a priori estimates. The unique sovablity is obvious, since we can first solve
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
by the theories of linear parabolic equations, see
[
25
]
and, then, derive
u
from
\eqref{linearized}_{2}
, see
[
11
,
35
]
.

Firstly, the sup-bound and lower order estimates of
\rho
has been proved in Section
3
. See Lemma
3.1
and
3.3
, that is,

(5.2)

\alpha\leq\rho\leq\beta,\quad\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C(\Omega,c)\,\,(\text{or }C(\Omega,c,\tilde{\rho})).

Next, we multiply
-\Delta\rho
on
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
and integrate over
\Omega
, then, using Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\left(|\Phi|+|\nabla\varphi|\right)|\nabla\rho||\Delta\rho|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Thus,

(5.3)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By virtue of Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality, we derive

(5.4)

\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\nu\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq\left\|\nabla\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\exp\left\{C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\,ds\right\}.

To get the higher order bounds, if
n\cdot\nabla\rho=0
on
\partial\Omega
, we apply
-\nabla\Delta\rho\nabla
on both sides of
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
and integrate over
\Omega
, then

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta\rho\right|^{2}+\int\varphi^{-1}\left|\nabla\Delta\rho\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\nabla\Delta\rho\cdot\nabla\Phi\cdot\nabla\rho+\int\Phi\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho

\displaystyle-\int 2\varphi^{-3}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}\nabla\rho\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho+\int\varphi^{-2}\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho

\displaystyle+\int\varphi^{-2}\nabla\rho\cdot\nabla^{2}\varphi\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho-\int\varphi^{-2}\Delta\rho\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho.

Then, applying Lemma
2.1
and Poincar e’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|\nabla\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{8}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{W^{1,4}}^{4}+\left\|\varphi\right\|_{W^{2,4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{8}}^{8}\right)\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

that is

(5.5)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{W^{1,4}}^{4}+\left\|\varphi\right\|_{W^{2,4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{8}}^{8}\right)\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality, leads to

(5.6)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\nu\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\Delta\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{W^{1,4}}^{4}+\left\|\varphi\right\|_{W^{2,4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{8}}^{8}\right)\,ds\right\}.

For
\rho
satisfying the non-homogeneous Dirichlet condition, that is,
\rho|_{\partial\Omega}=\tilde{\rho}
, taking
\rho_{t}\partial_{t}
on
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\left|\rho_{t}\right|^{2}+\nu\int\left|\nabla\rho_{t}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int\left|\Phi_{t}\right|\left|\nabla\rho\right|\left|\rho_{t}\right|+C\int\left|\varphi_{t}\right||\rho_{t}||\nabla\varphi||\nabla\rho|

\displaystyle+C\int\left|\rho_{t}\right|\left|\nabla\rho_{t}\right|\left|\nabla\varphi\right|+C\int\left|\rho_{t}\right|\left|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right|\left|\nabla\rho\right|

\displaystyle+C\int|\varphi_{t}||\rho_{t}|\left|\Delta\rho\right|.

Simiarly, it follows from Lemma
2.1
that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}+C\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|\Phi_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\Phi_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

that is,

(5.7)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Then, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and (
5.4
), one has

(5.8)

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\nu\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C(\Omega,c,\alpha,\beta,\Phi,\varphi,u_{0}).

Noticing that (
5.8
) also holds for the Neumann case.

Next, we take
L^{2}
-norm on
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
and use Lemma
2.1
to get

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and take
\nabla
on both sides of
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
to get

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{W^{1,4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{8}}^{8}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Thus, we use (
5.9
) and (
5.10
), alonging with (
5.4
) and (
5.7
), to deduce that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C(\Omega,c,\alpha,\beta,\Phi,\varphi,u_{0}).

In conclusion, for both cases, it follows from (
5.2
), (
5.4
), (
5.6
), (
5.8
) and (
5.11
) that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right)\,ds\leq C(\Omega,c,\alpha,\beta,\Phi,\varphi,u_{0}).

The next part is estimating
v
(for case (
A
) or (
B
)) or
u
(for case (
C
)). We first treat the case for
u
satisfying (
C
). Note that
\eqref{linearized}_{1}
is equivalent to

\rho_{t}+\dive[\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})]=\Delta(\rho\varphi^{-1}).

Thus, if we multiply
\eqref{linearized}_{2}
by
w:=u-Q
with
Q=\mathcal{B}[c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1}]
and integrate over
\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\rho|u|^{2}+\int 2\mu|D(u)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\Delta\left({\rho}{\varphi}^{-1}\right)\frac{|u|^{2}}{2}+\int\rho u_{t}\cdot Q+\int\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla u\cdot Q+\int 2\mu D(u)\cdot\nabla Q.

Then, using Lemma
2.1
and
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\left\|\nabla Q\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla Q\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\nu}{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

that is,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|Q\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

Next, for the estimate of
u_{t}
, multiplying
w_{t}=u_{t}-Q_{t}
on the both sides of
\eqref{linearized}_{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\int\rho|u_{t}|^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\int\mu|D(u)|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int\rho u_{t}\cdot Q_{t}-\int\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla u\cdot w_{t}

\displaystyle+\int\mu_{t}|D(u)|^{2}-\int\dive[2\mu D(u)]\cdot Q_{t}.

Using again Lemma
2.1
, applying Poincaré’s inequality and the fact that

\left\|Q_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right),

we obtain

(5.14)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\mu}D(u)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\mu_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{4}}+C\left\|Q_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|Q_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\mu_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

To estimate
\Delta u
, we change
\eqref{linearized}_{2}
into the form

-\mu\Delta v+\nabla p=2\nabla\mu\cdot D(u)-\rho u_{t}-\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla u+2c_{0}\mu\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1},

which, using Lemma
2.8
, leads to

(5.15)

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|p\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2},

that is, using Lemma
2.1
,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|p\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Then, using this bound together with (
5.14
), we have

(5.17)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\mu}D(u)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\mu_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

Finally, combining (
5.13
) and (
5.17
), then, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and the bound (
5.12
), we obtain the a priori estimates for
u
.

For case (
A
) or (
B
), as we have said at the end of Section
1
, we convert (
L.P.
) into

(5.18)

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+\Phi\cdot\nabla\rho-\dive(\varphi^{-1}\nabla\rho)=0,\\
\quad\\
\begin{cases}\rho v_{t}+\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla v-\dive(2\mu D(v))+\nabla p\\
=c_{0}\nabla(\log\rho)_{t}-c_{0}\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}+c_{0}\dive(2\mu\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}),\end{cases}\\
\quad\\
\dive v=0.\end{cases}

Then, we can apply the energy arguements analogous to the case (
C
). More precisely, multiplying
v
on both sides of
\eqref{2231}_{2}
and integrating over
\Omega
, we have, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2]
,

(5.19)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\rho\left|v\right|^{2}-\int\dive{[2\mu D(v)]}\cdot v

\displaystyle=\int\Delta(\rho\varphi^{-1})\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}+\int c_{0}\dive{\left(2\mu\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right)}\cdot v-\int c_{0}\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\cdot v

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

where we have used Poincaré’s inequality for the last inequality. For the term
-\int\dive{[2\mu(\rho)D(v)]}\cdot v
, we directly use the results in Lemma
3.5
, that is, for case (
B’
),

(5.20)

-\int\dive{[2\mu D(v)]}\cdot v=\int 2\mu|D(v)|^{2}\geq\nu\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\text{ (use Lemma \ref{Lemma2.2})},

while, for case (
A’
),

(5.21)

\displaystyle-\int\dive{[2\mu D(v)]}\cdot v\geq\nu\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left(C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Thus, combining (
5.19
)–(
5.21
), in both cases,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Delta\varphi\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

which, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and (
5.12
), gives

(5.23)

\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C(\Omega,c,\alpha,\beta,\Phi,\varphi,\rho_{0},v_{0}).

Next, multiplying
\eqref{2231}_{2}
by
v_{t}
and integrating over
\Omega
, one has, using Lemma
2.1
,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\int\rho\left|v_{t}\right|^{2}-\int\dive[2\mu D(v)]\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle=-\int\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla v\cdot v_{t}+\int c_{0}\dive{\left(2\mu\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right)}\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle-\int c_{0}\rho(\Phi+\nabla\varphi^{-1})\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right).

For the term
-\int\dive[2\mu D(v)]\cdot v_{t}
, if
(\rho,v)
satisfies the condition (
A’
), we use the proof from (
3.53
) to (
3.60
),

(5.25)

\displaystyle-\int\dive[2\mu D(v)]\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)+\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle-C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\mu_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1\right)\left\|v\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle-C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\nabla\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Recalling that

\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)=\int_{\partial}\mu v\cdot B\cdot v,\quad\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)=\int c_{0}\mu\nabla^{\perp}(v\cdot n^{\perp})\cdot B\cdot\nabla\rho^{-1}.

For case (
B’
), it is much easier,

(5.26)

\displaystyle-\int\dive[2\mu D(v)]\cdot v_{t}

\displaystyle=\int\mu\frac{d}{dt}|D(v)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\int\mu|D(v)|^{2}-\int\mu_{t}|D(v)|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{dt}\int\mu|D(v)|^{2}-\left(C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\mu_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right).

Furthermore, from (
5.15
), we have

(5.27)

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|p\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\log\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Therefore, combining (
5.24
)–(
5.27
), without loss of generality, one has

(5.28)

\displaystyle\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(t)+\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\mu_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1\right)\left\|v\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Phi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mu\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla\log\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Finally, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality, (
5.12
) and (
5.23
), we deduce that

(5.29)

\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C(\Omega,c,\alpha,\beta,\Phi,\varphi,\rho_{0},v_{0}).

Therefore, we complete the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

5.2
Preliminary Reductions

We claim that it is enough to prove the existence results for smooth initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
satisfying the compatiblity conditions (
1.11
). Once this is established, for general data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
, we can build a sequence of smooth initial data
(\rho_{0}^{n},u_{0}^{n})
such that it converges to
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
in some appropriate functional spaces. Then, we can obtain a corresponding sequence of solutions
(\rho^{n},v^{n},\pi^{n})
(or
(\rho^{n},u^{n},\pi^{n})
), which is uniformly bounded with respect of
n
, satisfying the initial data
(\rho_{0}^{n},v_{0}^{n})
(or
(\rho_{0}^{n},u_{0}^{n})
). We may use the weak convergence method and compactness reults to deduce that
(\rho^{n},v^{n},\pi^{n})
(or
(\rho^{n},u^{n},\pi^{n})
) converges to
(\rho,v,\pi)
(or
(\rho,u,\pi)
) in some functional spaces. As a result,
(\rho,v,\pi)
(or
(\rho,u,\pi)
) will be the solution we expect, which proves our claim.

Now, we explain how we obtain such smooth data. We begin with
\alpha\leq\rho_{0}\leq\beta
,
u_{0}\in H^{1}_{\omega}
(the case
u_{0}\in H^{1}_{nd}
or
H^{1}_{0}
can be done analogously). First, as we have said in Remark
1.11
, we can derive that
\rho_{0}\in H^{2}
from the compatiability condition (
1.11
)

(5.30)

\begin{cases}\Delta\rho_{0}^{-1}=c_{0}^{-1}\dive u_{0},&x\in\Omega,\\
n\cdot\nabla\rho_{0}^{-1}=0,&x\in\partial\Omega.\end{cases}

Consequently, we get
v_{0}\in H^{1}
by setting
v_{0}=u_{0}-c_{0}\nabla\rho_{0}^{-1}
. Then, we can construct a smooth sequence
(\hat{\rho}^{n}_{0},\hat{v}^{n}_{0})\in[C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})]^{4}
via flatten method and partition of unity such that

(5.31)

\begin{gathered}\hat{\rho}^{n}_{0}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\rho_{0}\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,H^{2},\quad\hat{v}_{0}^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}v_{0}\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,H^{1}.\end{gathered}

For details, see
[
15
]
Chapter 5.

However, the sequence
(\hat{\rho}^{n}_{0},\hat{v}_{0}^{n})
may be failed to satisfy the boundary conditions and divergence-free condition, which means that we need further construction. First of all, we solve the following ellptic problem

\begin{cases}\Delta\rho^{n}_{0}=\Delta\hat{\rho}^{n}_{0},&x\in\Omega,\\
n\cdot\nabla\rho^{n}_{0}=0,&x\in\partial\Omega,\\
(\rho_{0}^{n})_{\Omega}=(\rho_{0})_{\Omega}.\end{cases}

Of course, for each
n\geq 1
,
\rho_{0}^{n}\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
is unique and

(5.32)

\left\|\nabla(\rho^{n}_{0}-\rho^{m}_{0})\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\left\|\nabla(\hat{\rho}^{n}_{0}-\hat{\rho}^{m}_{0})\right\|_{H^{1}}\to 0,\quad\text{as }n,m\to\infty.

It follows from (
5.31
) that
\{\rho^{n}_{0}\}
is a Cauchy sequence, and, thus,
\rho^{n}_{0}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\rho_{0}
in
H^{2}
. Using Sobolev embedding theorem,
H^{2}\hookrightarrow C(\overline{\Omega})
, we deduce that
\rho^{n}_{0}
converges uniformly to
\rho_{0}
and thus, without loss of generality, we may assume that
\rho^{n}_{0}\in[\alpha,\beta]
.

Next, to construct
v_{0}^{n}
, we borrow from the construction method in
[
27
]
, Appendix A. More precisely, consider following Stokes problem of
v_{0}^{n}

\begin{cases}-\Delta v_{0}^{n}+\nabla p^{n}=-\Delta\hat{v}_{0}^{n},&x\in\Omega,\\
\dive v_{0}^{n}=0,&x\in\Omega,\\
v_{0}^{n}\cdot n=0,\,\,\curle v_{0}^{n}=-n^{\perp}\cdot\!B\cdot\![v_{0}^{n}+c_{0}\nabla(\rho_{0}^{n})^{-1}],&x\in\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where
\int p^{n}=0
and
\{\rho^{n}_{0}\}
is the smooth sequence we just obtain. In view of Lemma
2.5
, there exists a unique smooth solution
(v_{0}^{n},p^{n})\in[C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})]^{4}
such that

(5.33)

\|v_{0}^{n}\|_{H^{1}}+\|p^{n}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\hat{v}_{0}^{n}\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\rho_{0}^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}).

Thus, we obtain a Cauchy sequence

\|v_{0}^{n}-v_{0}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}+\|p^{n}-p^{m}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\hat{v}_{0}^{n}-\hat{v}_{0}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\rho_{0}^{n}-\rho_{0}^{m}\right\|_{H^{2}})\longrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\mathrm{as}\,\,n,m\to\infty,

because of (
5.31
) and the strong covergence of
\{\rho^{n}_{0}\}
. Without loss of generality, let

v_{0}^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\overline{v}_{0}\,\,\,\mathrm{in}\,\,H^{1}\,\,\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\,\,p^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}p\,\,\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{2}.

Then,
V_{0}:=\overline{v}_{0}-v_{0}
solves

\begin{cases}-\Delta V_{0}+\nabla p=0,&x\in\Omega,\\
\dive V_{0}=0,&x\in\Omega,\\
V_{0}\cdot n=0,\,\,\curle V_{0}=-n^{\perp}\cdot B\cdot V_{0},&x\in\partial\Omega.\end{cases}

It follows form the uniqueness of Stokes equations that
V_{0}\equiv 0
, that is,
\overline{v}_{0}=v_{0}
. Thus, we have found a smooth divergence-free sequence
v_{0}^{n}
, which satisfies the condtion (
A’
), that converges strongly to
v_{0}
in
H^{1}
.

If we treat the case (
C
), we just turn back to
u^{n}_{0}
by setting

u^{n}_{0}:=v_{0}^{n}+c_{0}\nabla(\rho^{n}_{0})^{-1}.

Then, it is easy to check that
u^{n}_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
,
u^{n}_{0}|_{\partial\Omega}=0
and
(\rho^{n}_{0},u^{n}_{0})
satisfies the compatiablity condition (
1.11
).

5.3
Approximate System

In order to get the existence for (
1.1
), we first try to establish the smooth solutions for the following system:

(A.P.)

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+v_{\eta}\cdot\nabla\rho-c_{0}\dive\left(\rho_{\eta}^{-1}\nabla\rho\right)=0,\\
\rho u_{t}+\rho u_{\eta}\cdot\nabla u-\dive[2\mu_{\epsilon}D(u)]+\nabla\pi=0,\\
\dive u=c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1},\quad\epsilon,\,\eta\in(0,1],\\
\rho|_{t=0}=\rho_{0},\quad u|_{t=0}=u_{0},\\
\alpha\leq\rho_{0}\leq\beta,\,\,(\rho_{0},u_{0})\in[C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})]^{4}\text{ satisfying }\eqref{compat},\\
u_{0},\,(\rho,u)\text{ satisfies one of the bundary conditions }\eqref{A}-\eqref{C}.\end{cases}

Let us give an explaination about the new elements in (
A.P.
). We define

u_{\eta}:=v_{\eta}+\rho_{\eta},\quad\mu_{\epsilon}:=\mu(\rho_{\epsilon}),

and
\rho_{\epsilon},\rho_{\eta},v_{\eta}
are constructed as we did in preceeding subsection, that is,
\rho_{\epsilon},\rho_{\eta},v_{\eta}\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
,
\dive v_{\eta}=0
,
\rho_{\epsilon},\rho_{\eta}\in[\alpha,\beta]
and
\rho_{\epsilon},\rho_{\eta},v_{\eta}
satisfying corresponding boundary conditions.

For convenience, we collect some bounds here which will be used later. Obviously, we always have
\mu_{\epsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
for every fixed
t
,
\epsilon
and, for all
1\leq r\leq\infty
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

(5.34)

\begin{gathered}\left\|\mu_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{r}}\leq C(r,\Omega)\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad\left\|\nabla\mu_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{r}}\leq C(r,\epsilon,\Omega)\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}},\\
\left\|\nabla^{k}\rho_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{r}}\leq C(k,r,\eta,\Omega)\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{1}},\quad\left\|\nabla^{k}\rho_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{r}}\leq C(k,r,\epsilon,\Omega)\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{1}},\\
\left\|\nabla^{k}v_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{r}}\leq C(k,r,\eta,\Omega)\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}.\\
\end{gathered}

Also, we have the following uniform controls, for all
1\leq q<\infty
,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\left\|v_{\eta}\right\|_{W^{\ell,q}}\leq C\left\|v\right\|_{W^{\ell,q}},\quad\ell=0,1,

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\eta}\right\|_{W^{\ell,q}}\leq C\left\|\rho\right\|_{W^{\ell,q}},\quad\ell=0,1,2.

Our aim is proving the following theorem.

Theorem 5.2
.

For every fixed
\epsilon,\,\eta\in(0,1]
, the problem (
A.P.
) admits an unique smooth solution on
\overline{Q}_{T_{1}}
for some positive time
T_{1}
.

Our proof is organized as follows. In the first part, we use iteration arguements and contraction mapping theorem to establish the unique smooth solution of (
A.P.
) for every fixed
\eta
and
\epsilon
. Then, we recover the original system (
1.1
) by letting
\eta
,
\epsilon
tend to
0
in turn with help of the uniform estimates.

5.3.1
Uniform Bounds

(5.36)

\begin{cases}\rho^{n}_{t}+v_{\eta}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\rho^{n}-c_{0}\dive\left[(\rho^{n-1}_{\eta})^{-1}\nabla\rho^{n}\right]=0,\\
\rho^{n}u^{n}_{t}+\rho^{n}u_{\eta}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla u^{n}-\dive[2\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}D(u^{n})]+\nabla\pi^{n}=0,\\
\dive u^{n}=c_{0}\Delta(\rho^{n})^{-1},\end{cases}

with the initial-boundary conditions

(5.37)

\displaystyle(\rho^{n},u^{n})(x,0)=(\rho_{0},u_{0}),\quad\text{in }\Omega,

(5.38)

\displaystyle n\cdot\nabla\rho^{n}=0,\quad u^{n}=0\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega\times(0,T).

where we use the following notations

\mu^{n}=\mu(\rho^{n}),\quad Q^{n}=\mathcal{B}[c_{0}\Delta(\rho^{n})^{-1}],\quad v^{n}=u^{n}+c_{0}\nabla(\rho^{n})^{-1},\quad w^{n}=u^{n}-Q^{n}

To prove the existence for (
5.36
), we construct approximate solutions as follows. We first define
(\rho^{0},u^{0})=(C,0)
and, then, assume that
(\rho^{n-1},u^{n-1})
was defined for
n\geq 1
, let
(\rho^{n},u^{n},\pi^{n})
be the unique global strong solution to the problem (
5.36
).

To prove the uniform bounds for the approximate solutions, we introduce the function
\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)
defined by

\mathcal{H}_{N}(t):=\begin{cases}\max_{1\leq n\leq N}\left(1+\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),&\text{case }\eqref{A}\text{ or }\eqref{B}\\
\max_{1\leq n\leq N}\left(1+\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),&\text{case }\eqref{C}\end{cases}

Observe that, in all cases, it follows from the maximal principle and energy estimates that

(5.39)

\alpha\leq\rho^{n}\leq\beta,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C,\text{ for all }T\in(0,\infty).

Moreover, let
N
be a fixed large number, we have

Lemma 5.3
.

There exists a positive constant
C
depending on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\rho_{0}
such that

(5.40)

\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C+C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{3}\,ds,

for all
n
,
1\leq n\leq N
.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 2
. From (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v_{\eta}^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}.

Then, we integrate from
0
to
t
with respect of time and finish the proof of lemma.
∎

The next Lemma concerns with the uniform bounds for case (
C
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
(\rho,u)
satisfy the condition (
C
). There exists a positive constant
C
depending on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
u_{0}
such that

(5.41)

\displaystyle\left[\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C+C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{4}\,ds,

for all
n
,
1\leq n\leq N
.

Proof.

For the higher regularity, we apply
-\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\nabla
on both sides of (
5.36
) and, then, integrate over
\Omega
to derive the analogue of (
3.33
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\left|\Delta\rho^{n}\right|^{2}+\int\frac{c_{0}}{\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}}\left|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\cdot\nabla v_{\eta}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\rho^{n}+\int v_{\eta}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla^{2}\rho^{n}\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}

\displaystyle-\int\frac{2c_{0}}{(\rho_{\eta}^{n-1})^{3}}\left|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}\right|^{2}\nabla\rho^{n}\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}+\int\frac{c_{0}}{(\rho_{\eta}^{n-1})^{2}}\nabla(\nabla\rho^{n}\cdot\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n-1})\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}

\displaystyle+\int\frac{c_{0}}{\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}}\Delta\rho^{n}\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}.

Then, applying Lemma
2.1
, we can obtain the following inequality which is similar with (
3.35
),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|v^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\left\|\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla v^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla v^{n-1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\left\|\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla v^{n-1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\Delta u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\left\|\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

which gives

(5.42)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\varepsilon_{1}\left\|\Delta u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

Moreover, from (
3.39
),we also have

(5.43)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|u^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

To get the bounds for
u^{n}
, noticing that the mass equation can be written as

\rho^{n}_{t}+\dive(\rho^{n}u_{\eta}^{n-1})=c_{0}\Delta(\rho^{n}/\rho^{n-1}_{\eta}).

Then, we follow the proof from (
5.13
) to get, for all
n\geq 2

(5.44)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho^{n}}u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{3}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{3}\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{3}}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}+\varepsilon_{3}\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Similarly, for
u^{n}_{t}
, it follows from (
5.14
) that

(5.45)

\displaystyle\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}}D(u^{n})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|u^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{4}}+C\left\|Q^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|Q^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\Delta u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{4}}\left(\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{4}\left\|\Delta u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Combining (
5.44
)–(
5.45
), we obtain

(5.46)

\displaystyle\left(\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}}D(u^{n})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\rho^{n}}u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{5}}\left(\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{5}\left\|\Delta u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, it follows from (
5.16
) that

(5.47)

\left\|\Delta u^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\pi^{n}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq C\left(\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Plugging this into (
5.42
) and (
5.46
) and, then, combining two of them, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}}D(u^{n})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\nu\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{\varepsilon_{4}}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\varepsilon_{4}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Alonging with (
5.43
) and choosing
\varepsilon_{2}
small enough and
\varepsilon_{4}=1/2
, one has

(5.48)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}}D(u^{n})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2C\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

For simplicity, we denote by the above

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{P}^{n}(t)+\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Then, integrating over
[0,t]
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{n}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{4}\,ds\right)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,ds.

Using this recursive inequality for
\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,ds
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{2}+\cdots+\frac{1}{2^{n}}\right)C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{4}\,ds\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{4}\,ds\right)

and hence, turning back to (
5.48
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{n}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,ds\leq C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{4}\,ds\right),

for all
2\leq n\leq N
and, thus, for all
1\leq n\leq N
. Finally, using (
5.47
), we get the bounds for
\Delta u^{n}
and
\pi^{n}
which concludes the lemma.
∎

Next, we give the uniform estimates for condition (
A
) or (
B
).

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
(\rho,v)
satisfy the condition (
A’
) or (
B’
). There exists a positive constant
C
depending on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\rho_{0}
and
v_{0}
such that

(5.49)

\displaystyle\left[\left\|v^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|v^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C+C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{8}\,ds,

for all
n
,
1\leq n\leq N
.

Proof.

We still only give the proof for case (
A’
). From (
5.22
) and the proof of Lemma
3.11
, we get

(5.50)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho^{n}}v^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla v^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|v_{\eta}^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}_{\eta}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n-1}_{\eta}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|v^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla\Delta(\rho^{n})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and

(5.51)

\displaystyle\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}^{n}_{1}(t)+\left\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}^{n}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}^{N}(t)^{3}+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{2}\left\|\nabla v^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}^{N}(t)^{3}+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{2}\left\|\nabla v^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}},

while, for
\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}
, we apply Lemma
2.9
for

(5.52)

\displaystyle-\dive[2\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}D(v^{n})]+\nabla\pi^{n}

\displaystyle=-\rho^{n}v^{n}_{t}+c_{0}\nabla\log\rho^{n}_{t}-\rho^{n}[v_{\eta}^{n-1}+c_{0}\nabla(\rho^{n-1})^{-1}]\cdot\nabla[v_{\eta}^{n}+c_{0}\nabla(\rho^{n})^{-1}]

\displaystyle+\dive[2\mu_{\epsilon}^{n}\nabla^{2}(\rho^{n})^{-1}]:=F^{n}

to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\mu^{n}_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+1\right)\left(\left\|F^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta(\rho^{n})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\left\|\nabla\mu^{n}_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla v^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)\left(\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\log\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta(\rho^{n})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\left\|\nabla v^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Delta(\rho^{n})^{-1}\right\|_{L^{4}})+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)\left(\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{\frac{3}{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left\|v^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}},

which leads to

(5.53)

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)\left(\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}.

Substituting this into (
5.51
), we have

(5.54)

\displaystyle\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}^{n}_{1}(t)+\left\|\sqrt{\mu}\curle v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}^{n}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}^{N}(t)^{3}+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}\left(\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{6}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathcal{H}_{N}^{8}(t)+\varepsilon_{1}\left(\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

On the other hand, following the proofs of (
5.42
)–(
5.43
), one has

(5.55)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+C\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla v^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{4}+C\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|\nabla v^{n-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|v^{n-1}\right\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{6}+\varepsilon_{2}\left(\left\|v^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n-1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)

and

(5.56)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{3}}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)^{3}+\varepsilon_{3}\left(\left\|v^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n-1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

Therefore, combining Lemma
5.3
, (
5.50
) and (
5.54
)–(
5.56
) and, then, using the same recursive arguements at the end of the proof of Lemma
5.4
, we can obtain the desire bound (
5.49
).
∎

Remark 5.6
.

It follows from (
5.47
) in Lemma
5.4
that the constant
C
in (
5.41
) depends on
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, which indicates that we can only obtain the local existence for the case (
C
) with
\mu=\mu_{\epsilon}
, in particular,
\mu
being a positive constant. However, from the proof of Lemma
5.5
, since we used Lemma
2.9
to get the estimate (
5.53
), the constant
C
in (
5.49
) is independent with
\epsilon
and that is why we could extend the local existence for cases (
A
) and (
B
) to general viscosity coefficient
\mu(\rho)
.

In conclusion, we have the bounds

(5.57)

\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)\leq C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}_{N}(s)^{q}\,ds\right),\text{ for some }q>1.

Thanks to this integral inequality, we can easily show that there exists a time
T_{1}\in(0,T)
depending only on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
and
u_{0}
such that

(5.58)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{1}]}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t)\leq C_{0},

for some
C_{0}
independing with
N
. Therefore, we obtain the bounds, for all
n\geq 1
,

(5.59)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{1}]}\left(\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\int_{0}^{T_{1}}\left(\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,ds\leq C_{0}.

5.3.2
Convergence

We next show that the whole sequence
(\rho^{n},u^{n})
converges to a solution to (
A.P.
) in a sufficiently strong sense. Let
