\sigma^{n+1}:=\rho^{n+1}-\rho^{n},\quad a^{n+1}:=u^{n+1}-u^{n},\quad b^{n+1}:=v^{n+1}-v^{n},\quad c^{n+1}:=Q^{n+1}-Q^{n}

and

\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t):=\begin{cases}\left\|a^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},&\text{case }\eqref{C}\\
\left\|b^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},&\text{case }\eqref{A}\text{ or }\eqref{B}\end{cases}

\mathcal{Z}^{n}(t):=\begin{cases}\left\|a^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2},&\text{case }\eqref{C}\\
\left\|b^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2},&\text{case }\eqref{A}\text{ or }\eqref{B}\end{cases}

In addition, we always let
\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)
and
\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)
be generic functions associated with the bounds (
5.59
) such that

\int_{0}^{T_{1}}\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\,dt+\sup_{t\in[0,T_{1}]}\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\leq C_{0},

where
C_{0}
is the constant as in (
5.59
).

\underline{\mathbf{Case\,\,\eqref{C}}}
:

It follows from the linearized mass equation that

(5.60)

\displaystyle\sigma^{n+1}_{t}+v_{\eta}^{n}\cdot\nabla\sigma^{n+1}-c_{0}\dive\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\eta}^{n}}\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right)

\displaystyle=-b_{\eta}^{n}\cdot\nabla\rho^{n}-c_{0}\dive\left(\frac{\sigma_{\eta}^{n}}{\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}\rho_{\eta}^{n}}\nabla\rho^{n}\right):=G^{n}

where

\displaystyle\left\|G^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left(\left\|b_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\leq C\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}\left(\left\|a^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|G^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|b_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}+C\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{W^{1,4}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}\left(\left\|a^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla G^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla b_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|b_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\left\|(|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}|+|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n-1}|)|\nabla\rho^{n}|\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\sigma_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\left(|\nabla\rho^{n}_{\eta}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho^{n-1}_{\eta}|^{2}+|\nabla^{2}\rho^{n-1}_{\eta}|+|\nabla^{2}\rho^{n}_{\eta}|\right)|\nabla\rho^{n}|\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|(|\nabla\rho^{n}_{\eta}|+|\nabla\rho^{n-1}_{\eta}|)|\nabla^{2}\rho^{n}|\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}\left(\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\rho^{n}\right\|_{W^{1,4}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|a^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C_{\eta}\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}.\quad\quad\quad\text{use }\eqref{base}

Then, using the simplified notations, the above bounds can be written as follows

(5.61)

\begin{gathered}\left\|G^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}+\left\|G^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t),\\
\left\|\nabla G^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\eta}\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)+C_{\eta}\sqrt{\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)}\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}.\end{gathered}

Next, we are going to establish the bounds for
\sigma^{n+1}
and
a^{n+1}
. Multiplying (
5.60
) by
\sigma^{n+1}
and integrating over
\Omega
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|G^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}},

then, using (
5.61
), we deduce that

(5.62)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|G^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t).

Similar with (
5.3
), multiplying
-\Delta\sigma^{n+1}
on both sides of (
5.60
) and integrating over
\Omega
, one has

(5.63)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|G^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t),

where we have used (
5.61
) for the last inequality.
If we integrate (
5.63
) between
[0,t]
,
t<T_{1}
, we have

(5.64)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,ds\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds.

For
\rho^{n}
satisfying the Neumann condition, we copy the proof from (
5.5
) by applying
-\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\nabla
on (
5.60
), integrating over
\Omega
and using (
5.61
), that is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{W^{1,4}}^{4}+\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{W^{2,4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{8}}^{8}\right)\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla G^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)+C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

This, alonging with (
5.64
), implies that

(5.65)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)+C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds.

For
\sigma^{n+1}_{t}
, we multiply
\sigma^{n+1}_{t}
on the both sides of (
5.60
) and integrating over
\Omega
, it follows analogously from (
5.7
) that

(5.66)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|u^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|a^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|a_{t}^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)

On the other hand, we differeniate the equations between those of
u^{n+1}
and
u^{n}
to get

(5.67)

\displaystyle\rho^{n+1}a^{n+1}_{t}+\rho^{n+1}u_{\eta}^{n}\cdot\nabla a^{n+1}-\dive[2\mu_{\eta}^{n+1}D(a^{n+1})]+\nabla(\pi^{n+1}-\pi^{n})

\displaystyle=-\sigma^{n+1}(u^{n}_{t}+u^{n}_{\eta}\cdot\nabla u^{n})-\rho^{n}a^{n}_{\eta}\cdot\nabla u^{n}+\dive[2(\mu_{\epsilon}^{n+1}-\mu_{\epsilon}^{n})D(u^{n})]:=K^{n},

where

\displaystyle\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|u^{n}_{t}+u^{n}_{\eta}\cdot\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|a^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|u^{n}_{t}+u^{n}_{\eta}\cdot\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|a^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\Delta u^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\sigma_{\eta}^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\left\|\nabla u^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\sigma_{\eta}^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}},

that is,

(5.68)

\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}+\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{2}}+C_{\eta}\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left\|a^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}},

Next, following the proof of (
5.13
), we multilpy
a^{n+1}-c^{n+1}
on both sides of (
5.67
), integrate over
\Omega
and use (
5.68
) to obtain

(5.69)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho^{n+1}}a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|u^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\left\|c^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|c^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|a^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\right)+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left\|a^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|a^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Here, for the last inequality, we have used Lemma
2.1
and

\displaystyle\left\|c^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{W^{1,p}},

\displaystyle\left\|c^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{8}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}+C\left\|\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

To get the higher bound for
a^{n}
, multiplying (
5.67
) by
a^{n+1}_{t}-c^{n+1}_{t}
, it follows from (
5.14
) that

(5.70)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|a^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|u^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{n+1}_{\eta,t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+C\left\|c^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|c^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\Delta a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left\|a^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|c^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\rho^{n}_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}+C\left\|\nabla\rho^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{4}}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+C\sqrt{\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)}\left(\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right).

At last, in order to get the estimate of
\Delta a^{n+1}
, we use (
5.16
) with the additional term
K^{n}
,

(5.71)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\pi^{n+1}-\pi^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|a^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta[(\rho^{n+1})^{-1}-(\rho^{n})^{-1}]\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|u_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|a^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\quad\text{use }\eqref{0570}

\displaystyle+C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left\|a^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

We substitute above into (
5.70
) to get

(5.72)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|a^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|\nabla a^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left\|a^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

Therefore, combining (
5.62
)–(
5.63
), (
5.65
)–(
5.66
), (
5.69
) and (
5.72
), we eventually get

(5.73)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)+\nu\mathcal{Z}^{n+1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)+\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)+\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds\right).

Then, applying the Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and recalling that
\mathcal{Y}^{n}(0)=0
and the definitions of
\mathcal{I}^{n}(t),\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)
, one has, for all
t\in(0,T_{1})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\left(C\mathcal{B}^{n}(s)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds+C\mathcal{I}^{n}(s)\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(\tau)\,d\tau\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds,

which reduces to the Volterra-type integral equation. After a simple recursive argument, we can show that

(5.74)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{1}]}\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)\leq C\frac{(CT_{1})^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}\int_{0}^{T_{1}}\mathcal{Y}^{1}(t)\,dt.

Applying the contraction mapping theorem and using this inequality together with (
5.73
), we show that the sequence
(\rho^{n},u^{n})
converges strongly to an unique limit
(\rho,u)
and, as a consequence,
\pi^{n}
converges strongly to a function
\pi
. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\rho^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\rho\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,C([0,T];H^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{3}),

\displaystyle u^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}u\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,C([0,T];H^{1})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}),

\displaystyle u_{t}^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}u_{t}\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle\pi^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\pi\quad\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}).

Of course,
(\rho,u,\pi)
is the unique strong solution in
\Omega\times(0,T_{1})
for (
A.P.
). Furthermore, we can show
(\rho,u,\pi)
is acually smooth. Indeed, sicne
u\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{2})\cap H^{1}(0,T;L^{2})
,
v_{\eta},\rho_{\eta}\in H^{1}(0,T;H^{\infty})
. With this regularity on
v_{\eta},\rho_{\eta}
, using the regularity theories of parabolic equations for
\eqref{origin}_{1}
, we can derive that
\rho\in H^{2}([0,T];H^{\infty})
. Then, applying the
L^{p}
-theory (
[
35
]
) for
\eqref{origin}_{2}
, we get
u\in H^{2}(0,T;H^{\infty})
and, hence, we can bootstrap and gain more time regualrity on
v_{\eta},\rho_{\eta}
then
\rho
, which implies that
(\rho,u)\in C^{\infty}(\overline{Q}_{T})
. Therefore, we finish the proof of Theorem
5.2
.

\underline{\mathbf{Case\,\,\eqref{A}\,\,or\,\,\eqref{B}}}
:

We only consider the case (
A
) here and case (
B
) can be proved identically. Firstly, it follows from (
5.68
) that

(5.75)

\displaystyle\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}+\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{2}}+C_{\eta}\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)(\left\|b^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}).

Then, applying the estimates (
5.22
) and (
5.28
) with
\varphi=\rho_{\eta}^{n}
and
\Phi=v_{\eta}^{n}
and using (
5.75
), we can obtain

(5.76)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\sqrt{\rho^{n+1}}b^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla b^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\Delta\rho^{n+1}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n+1}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\mu^{n+1}_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|v^{n}_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\Delta\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\left\|\nabla\Delta[(\rho^{n+1})^{-1}-(\rho^{n})^{-1}]\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|b^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)

and

(5.77)

\displaystyle\left\|b^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathcal{M}^{n}_{1}(t)+\left\|\nabla b^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}^{n}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|v_{\eta}^{n}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\left\|\mu^{n+1}_{\epsilon,t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mu^{n+1}_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1\right)\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|v_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mu^{n+1}_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)\left\|\Delta[(\rho^{n+1})^{-1}-(\rho^{n})^{-1}]\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Delta[(\rho^{n+1})^{-1}-(\rho^{n})^{-1}]\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla(\log\rho^{n+1}-\log\rho^{n})_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla[(\rho^{n+1})^{-1}-(\rho^{n})^{-1}]_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left\|K^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right)+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Delta\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\left(\left\|b^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma^{n}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right),

where

\mathcal{M}^{n}_{1}(t):=\int_{\partial}\mu_{\epsilon}^{n+1}b^{n+1}\cdot B\cdot b^{n+1},

\mathcal{M}^{n}_{2}(t):=\int c_{0}\mu_{\epsilon}^{n+1}\nabla^{\perp}(b^{n+1}\cdot n^{\perp})\cdot B\cdot\nabla\left[(\rho^{n+1})^{-1}-(\rho^{n})^{-1}\right].

Therefore, combining (
5.62
)–(
5.63
), (
5.65
)–(
5.66
), (
5.76
)–(
5.77
), we have

(5.78)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)+\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{M}^{n}_{2}(t)+\nu\mathcal{Z}^{n+1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)+\mathcal{B}^{n}(t)\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)+\mathcal{I}^{n}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds\right).

Here, we have used the fact that
\mathcal{M}^{n}_{1}(t)\geq 0
.

Noticing that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}^{n}_{2}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)+\left\|\nabla\rho^{n}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}(t)\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\sigma^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|b^{n+1}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(t)+C_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(t)\quad\text{use }\eqref{ee660}

Applying Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and using (
5.78
), we finally get the Volterra-type integral equation

(5.79)

\mathcal{Y}^{n+1}(t)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{Y}^{n}(s)\,ds,\quad t\in(0,T_{1}),

and, hence, following the proof of case (
C
), we complete the proof for the case (
A
).

In conclusion, we finish the proof for Theorem
5.2
.

5.4
Proofs of Theorem
1.6
: Recover
\epsilon
and
\eta

We temporarily fix
\epsilon\in(0,1]
to let
\eta\to 0^{+}
. We still first consider the case (
C
).

The recovering process is standard. Using Theorem
5.2
, we get a smooth sequence

(\rho^{\epsilon,\eta},u^{\epsilon,\eta},\pi^{\epsilon,\eta})\in C^{\infty}(\overline{Q}_{T_{1}})

which solves the problem (
A.P.
) for each
\epsilon,\,\eta\in(0,1]
(for simplicity, we use the notation
(\rho^{\eta},u^{\eta},\pi^{\eta})
). Next, we can follow the proofs in Lemma
5.3
–
5.4
step by step and the uniform bounds (
5.35
) to obtain the following control

(5.80)

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}^{\eta}(t)+\nu\mathcal{G}^{\eta}(t)\leq C\mathcal{F}^{\eta}(t)^{3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\eta}(t):=\left\|u^{\eta}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{\eta}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{\eta}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\eta}(t):=\left\|u^{\eta}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u^{\eta}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{\eta}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{\eta}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

and
C
is a constant which is not depend on
\eta
. Using the inequality (
5.80
), we can easily deduce that there eixsts a positive time
T_{2}
such that

(5.81)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{2}]}\mathcal{F}^{\eta}(t)+\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\mathcal{G}^{\eta}(t)\,dt\leq C_{2}.

Therefore, using the above uniform esitmate and Lemma
2.10
, we can derive that
(\rho^{\eta},u^{\eta},\pi^{\eta})
converges in some proper sense to the limit
(\rho,u,\pi)
such that

(5.82)

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+\dive(\rho u)=0,\\
\rho u_{t}+\rho u\cdot\nabla u-\dive[2\mu_{\epsilon}D(u)]+\nabla\pi=0,\\
\dive u=c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1}.\end{cases}

The convergence is easy to check, we left it to the reader. Of course, as a special case, we can let
\mu_{\epsilon}
be a constant
\mu
and, thus, we have proved the uniqueness and existence of the local strong solutions for the case (
C
).

For the case (
A
) or (
B
), the proof is basically the same. However, the difference lies in this case is that we can recover
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
because of the uniform estimates of
(\rho^{\epsilon},u^{\epsilon},\pi^{\epsilon})
,
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, see Remark
5.6
. The convergence is easy to check and we omit it. Thus, we have completed the proof of the existence results for Theorem
1.6
.

It remains to check the uniqueness for the case (
A
) or (
B
). However, this can be done by following the proof in
5.3.2
. Indeed, for example, if we consider the case (
A
) (another case can be proved analogously), let
(\rho_{i},u_{i},\pi_{i})
,
i=1,2
, be two strong solutions on
\Omega\times(0,T_{1})
with same initial data and set

\sigma:=\rho_{1}-\rho_{2},\quad a:=u_{1}-u_{2},\quad b:=v_{1}-v_{2},\quad c:=Q_{1}-Q_{2},

\mathcal{Y}(t):=\left\|a\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\quad\mathcal{Z}(t):=\left\|a_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Then, we can derive the similar type of equations to (
5.60
) and (
5.67
), that is,

\begin{cases}\sigma_{t}+v_{2}\cdot\nabla\sigma-c_{0}\dive\left(\rho_{2}^{-1}\nabla\sigma\right)=-b\cdot\nabla\rho_{2}-c_{0}\dive\left(\sigma\rho_{1}^{-1}\rho_{2}^{-1}\nabla\rho_{2}\right),\\
\rho_{1}a_{t}+\rho_{1}u_{1}\cdot\nabla a-\dive[2\mu D(a)]+\nabla(\pi_{1}-\pi_{2})=-\sigma(u_{1,t}+u_{1}\cdot\nabla u_{1})-\rho_{2}a\cdot\nabla u_{1}.\end{cases}

Applying the same discussions from
5.3.2
, we can get the following type inequality

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{Y}(t)+\mathcal{Z}(t)\leq C\mathcal{I}(t)\mathcal{Y}(t),

where
\mathcal{I}
stands for some integrable functions on time interval
(0,T_{1})
. Thus, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and the fact that
\mathcal{Y}(0)=0
, we can easily deduce that
\mathcal{Y}(t)\equiv 0
, which yields the uniqueness.

6
Proof of Theorem
1.3
–
1.5

6.1
Proof of Theorem
1.4
–
1.5

Since we have already show the existence and uniqueness of strong solution on
\Omega\times(0,T_{1})
for some positive times
T_{1}
, the proof of global ones is quite standard with the a priori estiamtes we obtained in Section
3
–
4
. One thing we should mention is that there is a gap between the local existence and the global one when
(\rho,u)
satisfies the condition (
C
) in that we only established the unique local strong solution for
\mu=\mu_{\epsilon}
. In every case that follows, one should first recover
\epsilon\to 0^{+}
to get the global existence and, then, show their uniqueness under the smallness assumption
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta
. Fourtunately, the proof of either is simpe and indentical with that in subsection
5.4
. The only thing one should notice is that, under the restriction
\left\|\nabla u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta
, Proposition
4.1
holds and, thus, we always have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\leq 1,

which allows us to use Lemma
2.9
(in such case, there is no difference between the estimates of Lemma
2.8
and those of Lemma
2.9
) and get the uniqueness.

6.2
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Following the construction process in subsection
5.2
, one can find a smooth sequence
(\rho_{0}^{n},v_{0}^{n})
such that

(6.1)

\begin{gathered}\rho_{0}^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\rho_{0}\quad\text{in}\,\,H^{1},\quad\alpha\leq\rho_{0}^{n}\leq\beta,\\
v_{0}^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}v_{0}\quad\text{in}\,\,L^{2},\quad\dive v_{0}^{n}=0,\quad v_{0}^{n}\cdot n=0\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega,\\
(\rho^{n}_{0},v_{0}^{n})\text{ satisfying }\eqref{A'}\text{ or }\eqref{B'}.\end{gathered}

If we define
u_{0}^{n}:=v_{0}^{n}+c_{0}\nabla(\rho_{0}^{n})^{-1}
, it is easy to check that
u_{0}^{n}
is smooth and
(\rho_{0}^{n},u_{0}^{n})
satisfies all the conditions in Theorem
1.4
. Thus, by using Theorem
1.4
, there exists a sequence of global strong solutions
(\rho^{n},u^{n})
of (
1.1
) with initial data
(\rho^{n}_{0},u^{n}_{0})
. Then, using the uniform bounds we get from subsection
3.1
, extracting subsequences if necessary, we can derive a weak convergent subsequence satisfying

(6.2)

\begin{cases}\rho^{n}\xrightharpoonup{w^{*}}\rho\,\,&\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}),\\
\rho^{n}\xrightharpoonup{\,\,w\,}\rho\,\,&\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}),\\
\rho^{n}_{t}\xrightharpoonup{\,\,w\,}\rho_{t}\,\,&\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}),\\
u^{n}\xrightharpoonup{w^{*}}u\,\,&\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}),\\
u^{n}\xrightharpoonup{\,\,w\,}u\,\,&\mathrm{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}).\end{cases}

Next, we can apply Lemma
3.7
to obtain (
3.25
)–(
3.26
). With these hold in hand, one can immediately get

(6.3)

u^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}u\quad\text{in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}).

Indeed, since
v^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}v\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2})
, it suffices to show the strong convergence for
\nabla(\rho^{n})^{-1}
, that is,

(6.4)

\nabla(\rho^{n})^{-1}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\nabla\rho^{-1}\quad\text{in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}).

However,

\nabla(\rho^{n})^{-1}=-(\rho^{n})^{-2}\nabla\rho^{n}

and
\rho^{n}\xrightarrow{\,\,s\,\,}\rho\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;H^{1})
, since we have
\eqref{6..2}_{2}
–
\eqref{6..2}_{3}
and, then, use Lemma
2.10
. Therefore, (
6.4
) is an easy consequence of (
3.25
) and Egorov theorem.

Finally, using (
3.25
), (
6.2
)–(
6.3
), we can recover the weak solutions
(\rho,u)
for system (
1.1
) and complete the prove of Theorem
1.3
.

7
Proof of Theorem
1.7

In the last section, we come to prove the blowup criterion for
(\rho,u)
satisfying one of three conditions (
A
), (
B
) and (
C
). Let
(\rho,u,\pi)
be a local strong solution as being described in Theorem
1.6
and suppose that (
1.13
) or (
1.14
) was false, that is, for some
r
and
s
satisfying (
1.15
),

(7.1)

\lim_{T\to T^{*}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{s}(0,T;L^{r})}\leq M_{0}<\infty.

or

(7.2)

\lim_{T\to T^{*}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{s}(0,T;L^{r})}\leq M_{0}<\infty.

We also let
\tilde{C}
be a positive generic constant depending on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\beta
,
T^{*}
,
M_{0}
and
\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{H^{1}}
. Then, our goal is proving the following estimate.

Proposition 7.1
.

Suppose that (
7.1
) holds for
(\rho,u)
satisfying the condition (
A
) or (
B
) and (
7.2
) holds for
(\rho,u)
satisfying the condition (
C
). Then, one has, for all
T\in(0,T^{*})
,

(7.3)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|u\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\left\|u\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Before proving the proposition, let us show how to derive the blowup criterion in Theorem
1.6
from Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

For simplicity, we give the prove for the case when
(\rho,u)
satisfies the condition (
A
), since other cases can be proved identically. Note that
\tilde{C}
, in (
7.3
), is uniformly bounded for all
T\leq T^{*}
, so

(\rho,u)(x,T^{*}):=\lim_{t\to T^{*}}(\rho,u)(x,t)\text{ in the sense of }H^{2}\times H^{1}

satisfying the conditions imposed on the initial data, that is,
\alpha\leq\rho_{0}\leq\beta,\,\,u_{0}\in H^{1}_{\omega}
, at the time
t=T^{*}
. Furthermore,

\begin{cases}\dive u|_{t=T^{*}}=c_{0}\Delta\rho^{-1}|_{t=T^{*}},&x\in\Omega\\
u|_{t=T^{*}}\cdot n=n\cdot\nabla\rho^{-1}|_{t=T^{*}},&x\in\partial\Omega\end{cases}

Thus,
(\rho,u)(x,T^{*})
satisfies (
1.11
) also. Therefore, we can take
(\rho,u)(x,T^{*})
as the initial data and apply the existence result in Theorem
1.6
to extend the local strong solution beyond
T^{*}
. This contradicts the maximality of
T^{*}
and, hence, we finish the proof of Theorem
1.6
.
∎

7.1
Case for
(\rho,u)
satisfying (
A
) or (
B
)

In this subsection, we always let
(\rho,u)
satisfy the condition (
A
) or (
B
). Recall that it is also equivalent to require
(\rho,v)
satisfying the condition (
A’
) or (
B’
).

The proof for the first part of Proposition
7.1
will be separated into the following few steps. The key of the proof is obtaining the lower order estimates for
(\rho,v)
, that is,
(\nabla\rho,v)\in C([0,T];L^{2})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1})
, then, following the proof in Section
3.3
, the weak solution is automatically a strong one.

The first lemma is just the combination of Lemma
3.1
and
3.3
, we give it here for convenience.

Lemma 7.2
.

The following bounds hold for all
T\in(0,T^{*})
, that is,

(7.4)

\alpha\leq\rho\leq\beta,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq C.

The next crucial lemma gives the lower bounds of
(\rho,v)
, that is,

Lemma 7.3
.

Suppose that (
7.1
) holds and
(\rho,v)
satisfies the condition (
A’
) or (
B’
), then one has

(7.5)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Proof.

We first follow the proof of Lemma
3.4
, applying Lemma
2.1
and
7.2
, to get

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\left(\left|\nabla\rho\right|^{2}+\left|v\right|\left|\nabla\rho\right|\right)\left|\Delta\rho\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}\right)\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}_{L^{r}}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\nu}{2}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}},

which implies that

(7.6)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{s}_{L^{r}}+1)\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

On the onther hand, as we did in (
3.12
), multiplying
v
on both sides of
\eqref{equation3.2}_{2}
and integrating over
\Omega
,

(7.7)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\rho\left|v\right|^{2}-\int\dive{[2\mu D(v)]}\cdot v=\sum_{i=1}^{3}I_{i},

where
I_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
, as in (
3.12
). From (
3.13
), applying Lemma
2.1
,
2.2
and
7.2
, the second term on the left-hand side can be controlled by

(7.8)

\displaystyle-\int\dive{[2\mu D(v)]}\cdot v

\displaystyle\geq\underline{\mu}\int\left|\curle v\right|^{2}-C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\nu\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left[C_{\varepsilon}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right].

Following the proof from (
3.14
) to (
3.17
), since

(7.9)

\displaystyle|J_{1}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\partial}\phi(\rho)(n\cdot\nabla\rho)(v\cdot n^{\perp})n^{\perp}\cdot\nabla\rho\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\nabla^{\perp}[\phi(\rho)(n\cdot\nabla\rho)]\cdot\nabla\rho(v\cdot n^{\perp})+\int\phi(\rho)(n\cdot\nabla\rho)\nabla\rho\cdot\nabla^{\perp}(v\cdot n^{\perp})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{2}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{1}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{1}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

(7.10)

\displaystyle|J_{2}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\partial}\phi(\rho)(v\cdot n^{\perp})n^{\perp}\cdot\nabla n\cdot\nabla\rho\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\nabla^{\perp}\phi(\rho)\cdot(\nabla n\cdot\nabla\rho)(v\cdot n^{\perp})-\int_{\Omega}\phi(\rho)\nabla^{\perp}\cdot(\nabla n\cdot\nabla\rho)(v\cdot n^{\perp})\,dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\int\phi(\rho)\nabla^{\perp}(v\cdot n^{\perp})\cdot(\nabla n\cdot\nabla\rho)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{2}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

and

(7.11)

\displaystyle|J_{3}|

\displaystyle=\left|\int 2c_{0}\mu(\rho)\partial_{ij}\rho^{-1}\partial_{j}v_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\partial}2c_{0}\mu(\rho)\nabla\rho^{-1}\cdot\nabla v\cdot n-\int 2c_{0}\mu^{\prime}\nabla\rho^{-1}\cdot\nabla v\cdot\nabla\rho\right|

\displaystyle=\left|-\int_{\partial}2c_{0}\mu(\rho)\nabla\rho^{-1}\cdot\nabla n\cdot v-\int 2c_{0}\mu^{\prime}\nabla\rho^{-1}\cdot\nabla v\cdot\nabla\rho\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon_{2}\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

we deduce that

(7.12)

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

Similarly, for
I_{2}
–
I_{3}
, one has

(7.13)

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Substituting (
7.12
)–(
7.14
) into (
7.7
) and, then, alonging with (
7.6
) leads to

(7.14)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\nu\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{s}_{L^{r}}+1)\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\rho}v\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Finally, applying the Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality to (
7.14
), we finish the proof of Lemma
7.3
.
∎

Now, we can prove the first part of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

It follows from Lemma
7.2
and
7.3
that

(7.15)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|\rho\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Thus, by Lemma
2.1
, we get the bounds

\int_{0}^{T}\left\|(\nabla\rho,v)\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

This, together with (
7.15
), allows us to follow the proof of Proposition
3.8
step by step, since the lower order bounds are enough to deduce the higher ones, according to Lemma
3.9
and
3.11
(noticing that, the proofs of Lemma
3.9
and
3.11
are merely based on the smallness assumption we derived from Proposition
3.2
, that is,
\left\|\nabla\rho_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta
, without any additional restriction, see also Remark
3.10
). We omit the remaining proof here and leave it to the reader.
∎

7.2
Case for
(\rho,u)
satisfying (
C
)

Now, we assume that
(\rho,u)
satisfies the condition (
C
).
One should notice that condition (
7.2
) is also equivalent with

(7.16)

\lim_{T\to T^{*}}\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{s}(0,T;L^{r})}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{s}(0,T;L^{r})}\right)\leq\tilde{M}_{0}<\infty,

since
\rho
is bounded from above and below and the identity (
1.17
).

Our aim is proving the rest of Proposition
7.1
under (
7.2
). First, we give the following lemma, which concludes some results we need later. This nothing but a directly application of Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
7.2
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
(\rho,u,\pi)
be a local strong solution as being described in Theorem
1.6
. Then, Lemma
7.2
still holds. Moreover, under the condition (
7.2
) (or, equivalently, (
7.16
)), one has
\rho\in C^{\gamma,\frac{\gamma}{2}}(\overline{Q}_{T})
for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
and for all
T\in(0,T^{*})
.

Next, with help of the Serrin’s condition (
7.2
), one can get the lower bound of
\rho
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Suppose that (
7.16
) holds and
(\rho,u)
satisfies (
C
), then

(7.17)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Proof.

As we did in Lemma (
3.4
), applying Lemma
2.1
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{L^{r}}\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|v\right\|^{s}_{L^{r}}+1\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

that is,

(7.18)

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|v\right\|^{s}_{L^{r}}+1\right)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Thus, using Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality and Lemma
2.1
, we conclude the proof.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

With this Lemma (
7.5
) and condition (
7.16
), we deduce from
\eqref{equation3.2}_{1}
that

(7.19)

\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Now, we can prove Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

We start with (
4.11
)

(7.20)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\frac{1}{2}\rho|u|^{2}+\int 2\mu(\rho)|D(u)|^{2}:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}S_{i},

where
S_{i}
as in (
4.11
). Using Lemma
2.1
,

(7.21)

\begin{cases}|S_{1}|\leq C\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}},\\
|S_{2}|\leq C\left\|Q\right\|_{L^{r}}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{\frac{2r}{r-2}}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{s}_{L^{r}}+1\right)\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\\
|S_{3}|\leq C\left\|\nabla Q\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{3}}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\right)+\varepsilon_{3}\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.\end{cases}

Combining (
7.20
) and (
7.21
) leads to,

(7.22)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|^{s}_{L^{r}}+1\right)\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

Similarly, we deduce from (
4.14
)–(
4.17
) that

(7.23)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{\mu(\rho)}|D(u)|\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

By Lemma
7.4
,
\mu(\rho)\in C(\overline{Q}_{T})
, hence, we can apply Lemma
2.8
for (
3.61
) with
\Phi=-c_{0}\nabla\rho^{-1}
and, then, use Lemma
2.1
and
7.4
to deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|v\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1\right)\left(\left\|\nabla v\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|v_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which gives

(7.24)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1\right)\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+C\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

Plugging (
7.24
) into (
7.23
) and choosing
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we have, for some positive constant
\nu
depending on
\Omega
,
c_{0}
,
\alpha
and
\beta
,

(7.25)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{\mu(\rho)}|D(u)|\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left(\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

On the other hand, following the proof from (
3.33
) to (
3.35
), replacing
\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}
by
\left\|v\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}
, then, replacing
v
by
u
via (
1.17
) and applying Lemma
2.1
, one has

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|u\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+1\right)\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\varepsilon\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

Alonging with (
4.23
), we deduce that

(7.26)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{L^{r}}^{s}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\right)\left(\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\left(\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Combining (
7.22
), (
7.25
) and (
7.26
), then, applying the Gr
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
nwall’s inequality, condition (
7.2
) and Lemma
7.5
, we get, for all
T\in(0,T^{*})
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\Delta\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Then, we can turn back to (
7.24
) to get

\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\pi\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\,dt\leq\tilde{C}.

Therefore, we complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
.
∎
